# Known issues

Every row here is work this project WILL do. There is no not-going-to-fix status
and no deferral: a divergence from Linux is either fixed or it is an open row
waiting for its lane. A row that records a shape we chose on purpose still names
the work that makes it right, because "we meant to" is not a reason to leave
Linux behaviour unimplemented.

**This file is the only store of live work.** Any lane that finds an issue adds
its row HERE, to the section it belongs to, in the same PR that finds it. Any
lane that fixes one flips it to `FIXED <sha>` and MOVES it to
`scratch/fixed-issues.md` in the same PR. Rows are never deleted, only
relocated.

Per-lane drop files under `scratch/issues.d/` are abolished and the directory is
gone (C300 folded the last 77 rows back in). A row parked in a side file is a row
nobody reads: the fold step was skipped often enough that a third of the ledger
lived outside it, and six lanes each filed the same aarch64 stack-ceiling defect
without seeing the other five. A concurrent-lane conflict on this file is a
rebase round-trip and is worth it — resolve it by taking BOTH sides' rows, never
by dropping one.

Before filing, read the section your row belongs to. If a row already states the
fact, add your evidence to it rather than filing a second row: one fact, one row.

`Status`: `OPEN` | `IN-PROGRESS <branch>` | `FIXED <sha>`.

`Owner`: `NEXT` is the queue head. Those rows are claimed before any unowned
work and become `IN-PROGRESS <branch>` when the current integration finishes.

`Class` says what KIND of work the row needs, never whether it gets done:

| Class | Means |
|---|---|

`Sev`: `blocker` (merge gate) | `high` (wrong answer reaching userspace) |
`med` (missing surface) | `low` (hygiene, tooling, cosmetics).

## Open-work summary

This is a derived view of the live `OPEN` and `IN-PROGRESS` rows below, not a
second ledger. Regenerate it — never hand-adjust it — in the same change
whenever a row is added, moved, or reclassified:

```
awk -F'|' '/^\| *(OPEN|IN-PROGRESS)/{c=$3;s=toupper($4);gsub(/ /,"",c);gsub(/ /,"",s);print c" "s}' \
  scratch/known_issues.md | sort | uniq -c
```

Hand-adjustment is what made the previous table wrong by 70 rows: it read 339
against an actual 409, and understated `MISSING high` and `INFRA high` by
exactly the rows a lane would have picked up first. D551 regenerated it, and
F1178 regenerated it again after adding eighteen rows — the table it replaced
was 78 rows behind the file. F1185 regenerated it a third time after three
concurrent lanes each appended a table instead of replacing the one above, so
the file carried three stacked tables and a reader taking the first was 44
rows out. D564 regenerated it a fourth time after moving 33 f2fs rows the two
f2fs completion waves had closed without flipping (one of the 33 — the
`set_clock` row — was already marked `FIXED` but had never been relocated out
of this file, a pure process omission). Two more were flipped and then put
back: the machinery existed but nothing at mount reached it, which is the
failure mode this reconcile was supposed to catch, not commit.

| Class | blocker | critical | high | med | low | Total |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| COVERAGE | 0 | 0 | 11 | 68 | 66 | 145 |
| DEFECT | 2 | 4 | 26 | 69 | 67 | 168 |
| INFRA | 0 | 0 | 11 | 40 | 39 | 90 |
| MISSING | 1 | 0 | 49 | 143 | 115 | 308 |
| **Total** | **3** | **4** | **97** | **320** | **287** | **711** |

Never delete a row to make the list look shorter. A row with no owner is still a
row. Retired rows and folded duplicates live in `scratch/fixed-issues.md`.

| OPEN | MISSING | high | **The nftables evaluation context is never populated, so every expression that reads a subsystem breaks at run time.** F1185 implemented all 28 remaining expressions and 199 hosted tests drive them through an injected context, but the ONE production construction site fills in `hook`, `mark` and `set_lookup` and leaves `ct`, `route`, `socket`, `xfrm`, `tunnel`, `osf`, `synproxy` and `objects` as `None` with `meta` at its default. A `ct state established` rule therefore never matches, and `meta iifname` never matches, on a real packet. This is not a defect in the expressions — each correctly refuses to fabricate a value — it is missing wiring. The seam is the hook signature: `net::stack::install_nf_hook` hands the callback only `(ns, hook, pkt, family)`, so there is nothing to populate from. Closing it means widening that callback and implementing the eight access traits. | F1185. `crates/kernel/netfilter/src/eval.rs` `eval_in_with_mark` is the only non-test `EvalCtx::new`; a grep for `impl <T>Access for` over `crates/` returns nothing outside `nft_expr_tests` for all eight traits; the callback is installed at `crates/kernel/kmain/src/kmain/rootfs.rs:208`. | unowned |
| OPEN | MISSING | high | **Every nftables action an expression records is discarded.** `nat`, `masq`, `redir`, `dup`, `fwd`, `log`, `reject`'s answer, `tproxy`, `synproxy`, `flow_offload`, the `payload` set direction and `exthdr` set/strip all evaluate correctly and push an `Action` onto the context, and the hook then reads only `verdict`, `mark` and the counters — `ctx.actions` has no reader outside the crate. A `reject` still drops because the verdict is separate, but no answer is sent to the peer; an `snat` rule installs no binding at all. Reading these expressions as working is the specific mistake this row exists to prevent. | F1185. A grep for `ctx.actions` outside `nft_expr` matches only unrelated fields in `drv-rtl8125` and `ipc::signal`; `crates/kernel/netfilter/src/eval.rs` consumes `verdict`/`mark`/`packets`/`bytes` only. | unowned |

It is not `Pkt` linearity (F844's claim) and it is not `RecvBuf::push_payload` copying at input time (F852's claim). The deciding fact is in the ABI: a by-reference completion reports a byte OFFSET INTO THE CALLER'S REGISTERED AREA, so a payload can only be delivered by reference if it physically sits in that area. Nothing but a device writing into the bound queue's buffers puts it there. The reference agrees exactly — it delivers by reference only when the fragment is a pool buffer belonging to THIS instance's own provider, copies unconditionally otherwise, and a registration made without a device has no pool at all, so every one of its deliveries is a copy. Rewriting the stream receive queue to hold buffer references would therefore not produce a single by-reference delivery; it would be machinery with no caller. What is LEFT is the read granularity, and closing it is a redesign rather than an edit: the tree under `quota/tree/` uses whole-file BYTE OFFSETS as its currency (`find_entry` -> `usize`, `store_at(file, at)`, `free_entry_slot` -> byte offset), so per-block access means changing that currency to (block, offset) and giving the tree a block accessor — the reference's `read_blk`/`write_blk` pair over `->quota_read`/`->quota_write`. | F1226. | OPEN | MISSING | med | **A compressed f2fs file's raw clusters are never considered for in-place update at all, and the narrow refusal the reference applies has no site here.** F1225 re-verified the original claim and found it MISLEADING in both directions. The reference refuses in-place update only for a compressed file in `compress_mode=user` with the enable-compress flag set, and that flag is set only for the span of the recompress ioctl, whose work it does by re-dirtying pages and letting WRITEBACK re-place them. This build's `compress_file` rewrites clusters synchronously (`compress/rewrite.rs::rewrite_clusters` -> `store_cluster_shaped`), so no such span exists and no page is ever in the mapping while it is set. | OPEN | DEFECT | high | **A sleeping lock serializes rmap and mapcount transitions, so the unmap / COW / address-space-teardown path sleeps while holding spinlocks.** `rmap_aware_dec_and_maybe_free` takes the PMM page lock around the mapcount check and rmap detach, and `rmap.rs` takes it again in every `set_/clear_/get_` rmap helper. The shared scanner identifies every reported LUN, obtains its capacity with the required 10-byte/16-byte transition, and publishes direct-access units as `sd*`; the SG_IO handler preserves the native v3 completion contract, validates admission against the open mode and raw-I/O capability, carries BOT failure as CHECK CONDITION plus REQUEST SENSE, and accepts flat or vectored transfers. ## Kernel core

| Status | Class | Sev | Issue | Evidence | Owner |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| OPEN | DEFECT | med | **A ring-submitted futex wait OCCUPIES a worker thread for the whole wait, where the reference occupies none.** `IORING_OP_FUTEX_WAIT`/`FUTEX_WAITV` are correctly forced onto a worker (they must never park the submitter), but the worker then calls the blocking futex syscall and sleeps in it. The reference instead registers a futex queue entry with a WAKE CALLBACK and returns immediately without completing, so a thousand outstanding futex waits cost a thousand queue entries and zero threads; here they cost a thousand worker slots, and the pool is bounded, so enough outstanding waits starve every other deferred operation on every ring. Observable as head-of-line blocking, not as a wrong answer — the completions are correct when they arrive. Closes when the futex layer grows a callback-registration entry point that io_uring can drive without a thread. | F1189. `io_uring::dispatch::proc_ops::{futex_wait,futex_waitv}` call `sys_futex_wait`/`sys_futex_waitv` from `iowq/run.rs`; the worker pool is the one global bounded pool (see the io_uring worker-pool row). Same shape applies to `IORING_OP_WAITID`. | io_uring lane |
| OPEN | MISSING | low | **`IORING_OP_READ_MULTISHOT` is the one defined io_uring opcode with no handler, and the probe correctly refuses to claim it.** Every other opcode number below `OP_LAST` is now dispatched. A multishot read re-arms itself and posts a completion per delivery, so it needs the multishot re-arm path applied to the read family rather than a new decoder; the arming machinery already exists for poll and for the send/receive family. Recorded so the remaining gap is one named row rather than an absence. | F1189. `io_uring_abi::ops::op_family` returns `None` only for this opcode; `the_one_unimplemented_opcode_is_not_claimed` and `every_defined_opcode_has_a_family` both pin it, the latter with an explicit skip that must be deleted when it lands. | io_uring lane |
| OPEN | COVERAGE | med | **Nothing on this box can distinguish the two class numberings the `/sys/fs/selinux` transaction nodes accept, so the check for it skips.** `selinuxfs::nodes::transaction::file_tests::a_class_whose_two_numberings_differ_is_answered_in_the_published_one` scans the loaded Fedora `policy.34` for a class whose policy value differs from the kernel classmap value AND whose two readings produce different labels; several classes differ in value (`system` is policy 3, kernel 4) but none produced a different answer for the pairing tried, so the test prints `skipping` and asserts nothing. The numbering fix is pinned only by the synthetic-fixture test in the `selinux` crate. A policy that reorders classes relative to the kernel classmap is what would expose it, and none is in the tree. | B2263. Run with `--nocapture`: `skipping: no class on this policy distinguishes the two numberings`. The fixture test that DOES fail on the defect is `selinux::services::transition::tests::a_class_written_by_userspace_is_read_in_the_policys_own_numbering`. | unowned |
| OPEN | MISSING | low | **A `/sys/fs/selinux` transaction answer has no size ceiling, where the reference refuses one that would not fit the transaction page with `ERANGE`.** `sel_write_create` checks the rendered context against `SIMPLE_TRANSACTION_LIMIT` and returns `-ERANGE` above it, so a caller reading with the page-sized buffer libselinux allocates never gets a truncated context. Our answers are staged in a heap `String` with no limit and a reader with a short buffer gets a silent prefix, which is a malformed context rather than an error. Not reachable with any real policy — a context is far below a page — but the refusal is part of the contract. | B2263, read from `sel_write_create`'s `len > SIMPLE_TRANSACTION_LIMIT` arm. `selinuxfs::nodes::transaction::TransactionOps::write_file` stages `answer.into_bytes()` unbounded. | unowned |
| OPEN | DEFECT | low | **The `/sys/fs/selinux` request parsers refuse a trailing extra field the reference ignores.** The reference parses with `sscanf(buf, "%s %s %hu", ...)` and `"%s %s %hu %s"`, which stop once their conversions are satisfied and leave any trailing text unread, so `"scon tcon 6 extra junk"` is a well-formed 4-field create request with name `extra`. Ours splits on whitespace and requires the count to be exactly 3, or 3-or-4 for `create`, so the same request is `EINVAL`. Unreachable from libselinux, which never sends extra fields, and ours is the stricter direction — but it is a different answer to the same bytes. | B2263, `selinuxfs::format::request::{parse_access_request, parse_create_request, parse_validatetrans_request, parse_context_request}` against `sel_write_access` / `sel_write_create` / `sel_write_validatetrans`. | unowned |

The signature is identical in BOTH captured x86 occurrences, pre-fix and post-fix, down to the tid and the syscall count: `40 4147 systemd S execve nsysc=3 cputime_ms=0 ptid=1` beside `1 systemd S clone3`. `cputime_ms=0` says the child has never accumulated a full millisecond of CPU, so it blocks EARLY in `execve` — long before the `vfork_done` publication at the end of it — and the parent is a victim rather than the bug: parking a vfork parent until the child execs or exits IS the reference's behaviour. **The rest of the machine is healthy**, which is new and is what makes this tractable: post-fix, both CPUs tick (heartbeat ages 2-8 ms), `preempt_count` is `0x0` on the parked CPU, `resched=0`, `ttwu_pending=0`, `wake_list=0`, and every task in the registry is `S` with nothing runnable. So this is a LOST WAKEUP or a lock nobody releases inside early `execve` — not a spinning CPU, not a stranded deferred wake, and not a CPU that cannot be interrupted. **Checked and excluded:** the deferred-wake-list stranding B2244 suspected (the new per-CPU dump reads `ttwu_pending=0 wake_list=0` on both CPUs), and the `irq_exit` preempt-count underflow another lane reported (`preempt_count=0x0` on the parked CPU; the `0x10000` on the peer is that CPU's own hard-IRQ field while it runs the dump). **B2265 (this lane) made the answer arrive by itself and did NOT reproduce it.** The wchan instrument (#5561) is in place and would print for this exact task — it is `S`, `on_rq=n`, `on_cpu=n`, so `park_site::reportable` admits it — but the retained logs predate the instrument and the wedge is 1-in-12, so no capture names the sleep yet. Rather than boot for it, B2265 shipped the reference's `khungtaskd`: any task in an UNINTERRUPTIBLE sleep for `hung_task_timeout_secs` with no context switch now logs `INFO: task <comm>:<pid> blocked for more than N seconds. tid=… last-syscall-nr=… wchan=<file>:<line>`, unconditionally (no debug feature), and `tools/boot-smoke.sh` treats that line as a fail marker. Every candidate sleep in early `execve` is uninterruptible, so the NEXT occurrence names its own sleep site with no operator at the console and no extra boot. **Enumerated and excluded by reading:** there is no `cred_guard_mutex` analogue held across early exec — our `exec_update_lock` equivalent (`ThreadGroup::try_exec_update`) is a `try_write` taken LATE (`059_execve/x86_64.rs`, after the ELF load) and returns `restart_nointr` on contention rather than sleeping, so the shape that classically produces this cannot; `sys_clone` holds no lock across `wait_for_vfork_done`; the sleeping `Mutex`, `WaitList` and `wait_event` families all enqueue under the condition gate before testing, closing the lost-wakeup window. **Leading suspect, unproven:** `BlkState::acquire_turn` (`crates/drivers/drv-virtio-blk/src/modern/wait.rs`) parks with `deadline_ns = 0` — the ONLY untimed wait on the path early `execve` takes to read the exec image, and the only virtio-blk wait without the `IO_TIMEOUT_NS` deadline its twin `wait_for_completion` uses to poison a stuck device. Its condition (`!busy && pending.is_empty() && deferred.is_empty()`) is republished by exactly one `BLK_TURN.wake_one()` per event from two sites; a single lost or mis-targeted one strands the waiter forever on an otherwise idle machine, which is the observed shape. | OPEN | COVERAGE | med | **No gate in this repo compiles any crate's `cfg(target_os = "oxide-kernel")` code unless that crate is in `kmain`'s dependency tree.** `make feature-gate` passes `-p kmain -p <boot> -p <bin>` only (`tools/xtask/src/cmds.rs:95-107`), so it reaches a crate solely through the dependency graph; `hosted-gate` and `test-build-gate` are host-target and compile none of it. A workspace crate that nothing depends on therefore has its entire kernel-only surface type-checked by nothing. Demonstrated on `sunrpc/src/clnt/park_kernel.rs`, whose 60 lines of `sched` wait/wake integration no routine gate builds. The fix is a per-crate kernel-target check paralleling `tools/hosted-check.sh` - a repo-wide tooling change, so F1188 filed it rather than crossing the boundary from a filesystem lane. | F1188, positive control: `let _: u32 = "THIS IS NOT A u32";` appended to `park_kernel.rs` gives `error[E0308]` under `cargo check -Z build-std=... --target targets/x86_64-unknown-oxide-kernel.json -p sunrpc`, while `cargo test -p sunrpc` reports `98 passed; 0 failed` and `make feature-gate-x86` returns rc=0. Same class as the documented `cargo check -p <crate>` null gate, one level out. | unowned |
| OPEN | INFRA | high | **A lane's worktree was deleted from under it mid-push, and the resulting gate failure reads exactly like a code defect.** `/home/nd/oxide/kernel-B2253` lost its entire source tree while `git push` was running its pre-push gates; only `target/` was left, `git worktree list` no longer registered it, and the local branch was gone. The visible symptom was `pre-push: FAIL - a crate's test targets do not build on their own (make test-build-gate)` with `error: could not find Cargo.toml in /home/nd/oxide/kernel-B2253 or any parent directory` -- a message that names a gate and a crate (`drv-bochs`) and invites the pushing lane to go fix a crate's features. Nothing was wrong with the crate: `make test-build-gate` returns rc=0 on `main` AND rc=0 on the branch's own pushed commit (`test-build-check: PASS - 167 crates build their test targets in isolation`). Two rules already forbid this (`CLAUDE.md`: "A worktree belongs to its lane owner", and the removal preconditions); what is missing is any signal that distinguishes "your change broke a gate" from "your source tree no longer exists". A gate that cannot find `Cargo.toml` should say the tree is gone, not blame the last crate it was iterating. Recovery was only possible because the ref had already reached the remote and the objects live in the shared store. | B2253. `ls /home/nd/oxide/kernel-B2253` -> `target` alone; `git worktree list` has no B2253 entry; `git branch --list '*B2253*'` shows only `remotes/origin/...`. `make test-build-gate` rc=0 in `/home/nd/oxide/kernel` (main) and rc=0 in a worktree recreated at the pushed commit `74884e4a2`. | unowned |
| OPEN | DEFECT | low | **The serial-sysrq armed state is ONE GLOBAL word for every UART; the reference keeps it per port (`port->sysrq`).** A break arriving on one port and a key on another are the same armed line here, so two serial devices can complete each other's sysrq sequence — and a break on a non-console port arms the console. The cause is upstream of sysrq and outside this lane's files: `drv_serial::set_rx_prefilter` takes a bare `fn(u8) -> bool` with no port argument, so the callback cannot learn which port a byte came from and the state has nowhere per-port to live. The reference also serialises the field under the port lock, which the prefilter has no access to; today the single console UART's single receive ISR is what keeps it consistent, not the code. Fixing it means giving the prefilter a port identity — a `drv-serial` change. Bounded meanwhile by B2255's five-second deadline, which is what makes it low rather than the sticky-forever defect it used to compound. | B2255. `crates/drivers/drv-serial/src/lib.rs:43,48` (`set_rx_prefilter`, `deliver` — no port argument); `sched::diag::sysrq::rx::ARMED_UNTIL_NS` is a single `static`. Reference 7.2-rc4: `struct uart_port` carries `sysrq` and `sysrq_ch` per port, and `uart_handle_break`/`uart_handle_sysrq_char` touch only their own port's fields. | unowned (drv-serial boundary) |
| OPEN | INFRA | med | **The aarch64 boot has a ~40-second window in which it prints NOTHING to serial, so any log read during it looks exactly like a stall at `[sysrq] always enabled by the boot line`.** Measured: `[7.821]` is the last line before `[48.111] [BOOT-DEV-NULL]`, i.e. rootfs/init produces no serial output for 40s of a 58s boot. A snapshot taken in that window is 1334 bytes with 3 kernel lines and reads as a dead kernel; two separate readings of exactly that file were taken as evidence of a boot that stopped, one of them the premise of this whole lane. Not a kernel defect on its own, but the silence is what makes the `>2000 lines` rule necessary and it should be either narrated (one progress line) or documented at the harness. | B2254. `scratch/evidence/b2254/arm-attempt-1-pass.log` lines 12-13; the same file at t≈8s was 1334 bytes / 3 kernel lines and at t≈58s was 45053 bytes / 311 kernel lines, same boot. | unowned |

| OPEN | INFRA | med | **A `Failed to read SELinux context of ...: Operation not permitted` line from PID 1 is NEVER a kernel `EPERM`.** systemd calls `fgetfilecon_raw(fileno(fp), &ctx)` on a plain `fopen(path,"re")` fd, which returns **-1** on failure (not `-errno`), and systemd passes that return value straight into its `log_*_errno` slot; the logger normalises it to `1`, which prints as `Operation not permitted`. The real errno is in `errno` and is never shown. Recorded so nobody spends a lane hunting for a kernel path that returns `EPERM` on a `security.selinux` read — the reference cannot return `EPERM` there at all (`fs/xattr.c:137-143` returns 0 for the whole namespace; a label denial is `-EACCES`). No `O_PATH`/`O_NOFOLLOW` anywhere on that fd. | unowned |

### B1720-fsmount-real-vfsmount

| Status | Class | Sev | Issue | Evidence | Owner |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| OPEN | INFRA | med | **A `make feature-gate` can report GREEN for the wrong tree.** `cd <worktree> && (gate) & (smoke) & wait` parses as `{ cd && gate } &` then `{ smoke } &` — `&` binds looser than `&&` — so the second job runs in the shell's original directory, normally the MAIN tree. Observed here: the gate type-checked `main` and passed while this branch's kernel build was broken by a missing re-export (`E0425`), which only `make x86` caught. Not a defect in the gate itself (a correctly-targeted run catches it: exit=2 with the error) but a foot-gun that the newly-mandated parallel-checks rule makes MORE likely. `CLAUDE.md` now carries the shape and says to confirm the gate log names your worktree. A `make` guard that refuses to run when `$(CURDIR)` is not the git worktree root of the checked source would remove it entirely. | This lane, twice: gate-exit=0 with `Checking vfs (/home/nd/oxide/kernel/...)` in the log while the worktree was `/home/nd/oxide/kernel-B1720`; after the fix, gate-exit=0 with the worktree's own paths. | unowned |

(3) `memory_hugetlb_accounting`: no hugetlb exists in this kernel at all. (4) `favordynmods`: a locking-strategy choice with no user-visible semantics and no dynamic-modification cost model here. ### B1730-irq-nesting-unbounded

| Status | Class | Sev | Issue | Evidence | Owner |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| OPEN | MISSING | med | **No interrupt-nesting depth bound and no runtime stack high-water mark.** Nesting is now structurally bounded at two levels, but nothing measures or enforces it, so a future change that reopens a window fails the same silent way. The reference reports a stack's deepest use (`CONFIG_DEBUG_STACK_USAGE`: zero-fill at allocation — which `kstack::alloc` already does — then scan upward from the low end to the first non-zero word, reported at task exit by `check_stack_usage`). Adding that plus an explicit nesting counter would make a regression measurable instead of fatal. Design worked out this lane: scan in `Drop for Task`, one global low-water atomic, and an on-demand sysrq key for the interrupt stack, which is never freed and so has no exit point. | Reference: `kernel/exit.c` `stack_not_used`/`check_stack_usage`; `kernel/fork.c` `GFP_VMAP_STACK` zero-fill. Our zero-fill is already at `kstack.rs` `alloc`. | unowned |

### B1731-failed-units-boot-health

| Status | Class | Sev | Issue | Evidence | Owner |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| OPEN | DEFECT | med | **`systemctl` intermittently cannot reach the service manager: `Failed to list units: Transport endpoint is not connected`.** Seen once ~75 s into a live-gnome boot, from a shell that had worked moments earlier; a later run at ~40 s queried cleanly. ENOTCONN on the manager's AF_UNIX socket means the peer went away or the connection was reset, which on a healthy system does not happen. Recorded because it silently turns a verification query into an empty result — an empty `systemctl --failed` was briefly mistaken for "nothing fails". Any probe that reads unit state must check for this line before believing the answer. | `verify2.log`: `Failed to list units: Transport endpoint is not connected`, with the same command succeeding in `verify3.log`. | unowned |
| OPEN | INFRA | med | **Boot probes were used to answer questions the source answers.** Roughly eight boots went into this lane, several of them to establish facts a grep would have given in seconds, and two produced no usable answer at all because the typed probe was mangled by interleaved kernel output or swallowed by `systemctl`'s pager. The repo's own rule says a harness beats a boot and that more than two boots chasing one bug means the loop is the bug. Practical notes for the next lane: pass `SYSTEMD_PAGER=cat` and `--no-pager` or the probe hangs in `less`; the alive-marker must not appear in the typed command (use the quote-split form); and `rc=$?` after a pipeline reports the LAST command's status, not the one being tested — that mistake produced a false `rc=0` for three mounts here. | This lane. | unowned |

### D513-real-hardware-findings

| Status | Class | Sev | Issue | Evidence | Owner |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| IN-PROGRESS B2111-xhci-linux-audit | MISSING | blocker | **Native PCI xHCI now exists and is registered before PCI driver probing:** class `0x0c0330`, IOMMU-mapped DMA pages, controller reset/run, MSI/MSI-X binding, root-port address/configure, USB HID boot keyboard/mouse reports, platform-keyed evdev publication, and root-port connect/disconnect handling through the USB softirq. The old claim that no xHCI or bus-agnostic input existed is false. USB mass-storage descriptor discovery, Bulk-Only/SCSI CBW/CSW contracts, generic bulk endpoint-context construction, retained requester-keyed bulk DMA owners, non-overwriting transfer-completion handoff, native configuration sequencing, native CBW/data/CSW transfer submission, initial INQUIRY/READ CAPACITY transaction sequencing, canonical `sd*` block-device publication, and page-vector payload transfers now exist. One transaction can map up to 255 IOMMU DMA pages and submits chained Normal TRBs with a completion only on the terminal buffer. Root addressing now flows through an xHCI topology owner that preserves a physical root port plus route string. Hub-class status endpoints are parsed, configured, submitted, completion-validated, DMA-invalidated, and rearmed through that same transport owner; the hub descriptor is fetched in two stages, then its protocol/TT facts update the xHCI hub slot context. Hub initialization powers every downstream port, waits its one descriptor-provided power-good interval, reads changed/initial port status in a process worker, acknowledges change features, resets connected ports, and publishes descendant slots with the parent route string. The port reset-control transaction drops controller/device locks before completion waits and recovery delay. Descendant detach and recursive topology are still absent. The adapter owns the SCSI disk reservation until detach, rejects reuse when the canonical registry cannot detach an open disk, and serializes every command through the controller-then-device transaction gate. The shared block registry owns the reusable SCSI `sd*` identity allocator, so SATA and USB storage do not publish through competing counters; a shared SCSI midlayer, full hub topology, HID report-descriptor support beyond boot protocol, and physical xHCI exercise remain. The Linux module facade remains a shim and is not the native-driver path. | `drv-xhci::{probe,probe_hub,storage_block,irq,completion,usb,storage,context,hid,device}`; `block::{registry,BlockDevice}`; `pci-boot::register_pci_model_drivers`; `input::{InputDeviceKey::platform,install,publish_evdev}`; `irq::take_port_changes` → `probe::service_port_changes`. | B2111-xhci-linux-audit; B2112-usb-mass-storage; B211301-xhci-bulk-endpoints; B2113-xhci-storage-dma; B2114-xhci-completion-queue; B2115-xhci-storage-probe; B2116-xhci-bot-execution; B2118-block-sd-name-allocator; B2119-xhci-controller-ownership; B2120-xhci-controller-handle; B2121-xhci-storage-transaction; B2122-xhci-storage-block-device; B2124-xhci-storage-scatter-gather; B2125-xhci-hub-topology; B2126-xhci-hub-endpoint; B2127-xhci-hub-port-control; B2128-xhci-hub-port-status; B2129-xhci-hub-child-enumeration |

| OPEN | DEFECT | low | PTRACE_GETREGSET/SETREGSET pre-checks the tracer's `struct iovec` with `validate_user_buf_writable`, so an iovec in a read-only mapping is refused with EFAULT before any transfer runs. | OPEN | DEFECT | high | **`cmpxchg_user_u32` performs an atomic read-modify-write DIRECTLY on a user address with no exception-table fixup**, so the PI-futex and robust-futex paths still fault the kernel when the word goes away — the one remaining raw user dereference in `ipc` after B2054. It is reached from `futex/pi/{lock,unlock,requeue,exit}.rs` and `futex/robust.rs`, each of which guards it with a `user_addr_accessible(uaddr, true)` page-table probe. The probe does not close the window: the futex word belongs to a live multi-threaded process whose PEERS can unmap it between the probe and the RMW, and on the robust/PI exit paths the guard is all that stands between a hostile userspace and a kernel fault. The reference has a dedicated fixup-carrying primitive for exactly this (`futex_atomic_cmpxchg_inatomic`), which returns `-EFAULT` instead of faulting. The fix is an arch asm routine per HAL — `lock cmpxchg` with an `__ex_table` entry on x86_64, `ldaxr/stlxr` with one on aarch64 — surfaced through `uaccess` beside `raw_copy_{from,to}_user`, after which every `user_addr_accessible(_, true)` call in `ipc` can go. | `crates/kernel/ipc/src/live/futex/core.rs::cmpxchg_user_u32` builds an `&AtomicU32` straight from the user VA; `crates/arch/hal-{x86_64,aarch64}/src/uaccess.rs` contains only the two byte-copy routines, so nothing else in the tree has a fixup. | OPEN | DEFECT | med | **`uaccess` has no single-instruction `get_user`/`put_user`, so every scalar user access in the kernel pays a byte-copy routine — and on aarch64 that routine copies ONE BYTE PER ITERATION.** `oxide_raw_copy_from_user`/`_to_user` are `rep movsb` on x86_64 (acceptable for a 4-byte fetch, ~an order of magnitude over a single `mov`) and a `ldrb`/`strb` loop with a `subs`/`b.ne` on aarch64, which is byte-rate for EVERY copy in the kernel, not only the scalar ones — a `read(2)` of a page moves 4096 bytes one at a time. The reference has both: `__get_user`/`__put_user` are a single fixup-carrying load or store, and `copy_from_user` is a tuned bulk routine. Two separate wins: a `get_user_u32`/`put_user_u32`/`get_user_u64` family for the hot scalar paths (`futex_get_value_locked` is on FUTEX_WAIT, the hottest lock path in the system), and a word-at-a-time aarch64 bulk copy. | `crates/arch/hal-aarch64/src/uaccess.rs` — the whole body is `1: ldrb w3,[x1],#1; 2: strb w3,[x0],#1; subs x2,x2,#1; b.ne 1b`. B2054 converted `ipc`'s futex fetches to `copy_from_user` on a 4-byte stack array rather than add arch asm outside its lane; no bounce buffer is allocated, but the call is still the byte loop on ARM. | unassigned |

| Status | Class | Sev | Issue | Evidence | Owner |

| FIXED 85072ceaa | DEFECT | high | `/dev/loopN` reserved 16 minors per device while its ioctl bridge treated the raw minor as the loop number. Thus the published `/dev/loop1` at 7:16 resolved as nonexistent `loop16`, fell through to the generic ioctl owner, and made `udisksd` log `LOOP_GET_STATUS64: ENOTTY` for loop1 through loop7. The driver now publishes default loop devices as unpartitioned: raw minor `N` names `loopN`, matching the reference default. | `boot.txt` at 107.340--107.629: repeated `udisksd` loop1--loop7 `Inappropriate ioctl for device`; `drv-loop::LOOP_DRIVER` used `PARTITION_MINOR_COUNT` while `syscalls/016_ioctl/loop_dev.rs::device_of` passes `devt.minor()` directly to `registry::device`. `registry::default_loop_layout_uses_one_minor_per_device` is green, and fails when the old 16-minor layout is restored. | B2281-boot-compat-failures |

### B1979-gnome-segfault-regression-triage

| Status | Class | Sev | Issue | Evidence | Owner |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| OPEN | DEFECT | High | A process that loads the GIO module `libdconfsettings.so` intermittently dies on the FIRST read of the dconf database it has just mapped. The faulting instruction is `mov (%rax),%edx` at module offset `0x5245`, where `%rax` is the pointer `g_mapped_file_new()` → `g_mapped_file_get_bytes()` → `g_bytes_get_data()` just handed back and the size check `cmpq $0x17,0x10(%rbx)` has already passed. Older observations predate the fault-reason classifier and appear as `SIGSEGV`; the current `gsd-power` observation is `SIGBUS`/`BUS_ADRERR`, which proves the file-fill path returned an error rather than merely lacking a VMA. When the victim is `gnome-shell` the whole desktop dies to a bare console; when it is a session service (`evolution-source-registry` or `gsd-power`) the desktop survives. **Not introduced by the 2026-08-08 wave** — it reproduces on `5506b785e`, the last-known-good commit, at the SAME module offset. | Paired live-gnome boots, same host, same qemu invocation, run concurrently: `910470808` 0 faults / 5 boots, `5506b785e` 1 fault / 5 boots (plus one earlier `910470808` boot under a wider device set, which faulted — so 1/6 vs 1/5, indistinguishable at this sample size). Both older fault lines carry `ino 0x6e54000000003e14` (ext4 inode 15892 = `/usr/lib64/gio/modules/libdconfsettings.so`) and `off 0x5245`; only the victim process and the ASLR bases differ. Current `boot.txt` has `gsd-power[495]: bus error ... in libdconfsettings.so[...+0x5245]` at 113.052 s. `configured_root_dconf_databases_read_through_framecache` passes on the composed root image through ext4's `i_mapping` path, so the static system dconf databases are valid under the ordinary frame-cache path; it does not exercise live device I/O or a mutable per-user database. | B2281-boot-compat-failures |

The remaining cause is still not identified: it can be the frame store's inode read, extent/data read, or the underlying block request; a normal hosted read of the current static dconf databases is green. Closing it is a transmit-scheduling lane, not a socket-option one. | `net::netdev::tx_dispatch` orders by band only; `net::socket_error::queue::publish_txtime` has no non-test caller. | unclaimed |
| OPEN | MISSING | med | There is no transform subsystem, so a datagram the UDP encapsulation handler classifies as an encapsulated security payload is consumed and dropped instead of being decapsulated and re-injected. The classifier itself now matches the reference exactly (keepalive eaten, non-ESP marker passed through as control traffic, everything else consumed), so the row's original "stored with zero consumers" wording is no longer true — what is absent is everything downstream of the classification: a security-association table, the decrypt, and the re-injection. `UDP_ENCAP_L2TPINUDP` is in the same position with no tunnel subsystem. | `net::sock_opts::sol_udp::encap::rx_verdict` and its `tests_udp_encap` cases; nothing consumes the security-payload verdict. | unclaimed |

### B1985-fault-fill-failure-reports-sigbus

| Status | Class | Sev | Issue | Evidence | Owner |

That contract holds for a memcg charge failure (`mm-pmm/src/memcg.rs` `MemoryPressure::Max` reclaims, then `sched::oom::kill_memcg`), and does NOT hold for plain frame exhaustion: `alloc_one_frame` returning `None` kills nothing, so the instruction re-faults forever. Closing this means a global OOM selector on the allocator failure path, not a change to the fault classification. | `crates/kernel/mm-vmm/src/fault_signal.rs` `FaultFailure::Oom`; the only OOM selector in the tree is `sched::oom::kill_memcg`, reached solely from the cgroup pressure hook (grep: no other caller). Previously this reason terminated the task with SIGSEGV, which is not what the reference reports. | unowned |

### B1986-main-aarch64-stack-overrun

| Status | Class | Sev | Issue | Evidence | Owner |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| OPEN | DEFECT | high | **The socket destructor runs inline on whoever drops the last reference, so a 4,544 B teardown cascade is charged to every path in the kernel that can touch a socket.** (D551: `stack-gate` is GREEN on both arches as of 44765839c, so this is no longer a failing gate — it is a deep path with ~4 KB of headroom left, and the gate will fail on the next thing that lands on top of it.) `Drop for InetSocket` calls `release_file`, and from there: `release_file` 352 -> `tcp_fastopen::drain_client` 624 -> `net_ns::state::materialize_state` 1,168 -> `NsNet` / `UnixListener` / `UnixPair` drops -> `Arc<Task>` -> FdTable -> `filp_close` -> `File` -> mount -> `MntNamespace` -> `generic_shutdown_super` -> writeback. The reference does not do this: `__sk_free` hands `__sk_destruct` to `call_rcu`, so the destructor runs from an RCU callback in process context. **RETRACTION (B1996): the `in_interrupt()` branch in `release_file` is NOT a split truth and must not be "unified" away.** It is the reference's own distinction between close-time protocol shutdown (`inet_release`: process context, synchronous, takes RTNL) and final destruction from an arbitrary context (`__sk_destruct`: deferred). Making the deferral unconditional was tried and is WRONG — it breaks the contract that a close releases its device references immediately, and seven hosted tests assert exactly that (`final_close_flushes_every_unique_device_reference`, `process_context_final_drop_still_releases_inline`, `socket_anycast_is_not_multicast_and_close_releases_device_ref`, and four more). Closing this row is therefore NOT about when the destructor runs. It is about making the destructor path SHALLOW: the deep frames under it (`tcp_fastopen::drain_client` 624, `materialize_state` 1,168) have to shrink, or the owned state has to be moved out of the socket into a queued bundle so the drop itself is a hand-off. Routing individual `Arc<InetSocket>` drop sites through the reaper was also tried and does not work: the destructor edge exists at many sites (`packet_fanout::rollover` keeps it even after `packet::deliver` is routed), the gate got WORSE (13,024 -> 13,040), and the same seven tests fail. | B1995, measured on aarch64 at `621c7cfe8`: `Arc<InetSocket>::drop_slow` subtree = 4,544 B, reached on the ceiling path from `packet::deliver`'s upgraded socket references. It is larger than every net transmit frame on that path combined. | unowned |
| OPEN | DEFECT | med | **`net_ns::state::materialize_state` reserves 1,168 B of stack to insert one map entry.** The body is a `BTreeMap` lookup and an `Arc::new(NsNet::new())`; the frame is that large because `NsNet` is built as a stack temporary and then copied into the `Arc` allocation. It is the single largest frame on the aarch64 ceiling path and it is reached from a destructor. Any large structure constructed by value and then moved into an allocation has the same shape, so the fix is a construction pattern, not one call site. | B1995. `stack-depth-gate --show-path` on the `Arc<InetSocket>::drop_slow` subtree: `materialize_state` +1,168, against a body whose own work is O(log N) on a map. | unowned |
| OPEN | COVERAGE | low | Negative result, recorded so the next lane does not re-run it: the io_uring submission frames are at their floor and **splitting them makes the gate worse**, because the ~30 opcode arms already coalesce into one well-coloured frame. Marking `io_uring::dispatch::router::run` `#[inline(never)]` moved the deepest path 12992 → **13024** (FAIL); marking `dispatch_op` `#[inline(never)]` moved it to **13136** (FAIL, `submit_sqes` 416 → 320 + a new 240 B `dispatch_op` frame). Splitting the `oxide_syscall_dispatch` exit tail into an `#[inline(never)]` `syscall_exit_work` — the mirror of the `syscall_entry_work` split a prior lane made — was exactly neutral (12992, dispatch frame still 304), so the exit work was already overlapping the routes rather than summing with them. All three were reverted. | Three measured aarch64 builds; `submit_sqes` prologue is `sub sp, sp, #0x140` of which 96 B is the callee-saved spill, leaving ~224 B of genuinely-live ABI data (`Sqe` 72, `Op` 40, `SyscallArgs` 48, `SelectedBuf`/`ScratchFd`). | B1986 |
| OPEN | INFRA | low | The `known_issues.md` open-work summary was destroyed by a merge, not merely stale: the `Class \| Means` table's four body rows had been overwritten with count rows, the `## Open-work summary` heading and its "derived view" prose were gone, and two different count tables (260 and 270) were stacked under one header with no severity columns. Restored and recounted here. This is the third time in one day the derived view has gone wrong, and it goes wrong at conflict-resolution time — the failure mode CLAUDE.md names under "Conflict resolution is where coverage dies". ### B1989-membarrier-sync-core-and-rseq

| Status | Class | Sev | Issue | Evidence | Owner |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| OPEN | COVERAGE | MED | The expedited-round IPI protocol has no check that can fail. The round KIND is stored Release BEFORE `PENDING`; reversing the two stores lets a target run a plain barrier for a SYNC_CORE round and silently drop the core-serialization guarantee, and nothing hosted or booted would notice. The ACK wait and the single-in-flight `OWNER` handoff are equally unpinned. LANE SCOPE: model the sender/target pair under loom (the repo already uses loom elsewhere) — one sender publishing KIND+PENDING against one or more targets running `service()`, asserting that any target which clears its bit observed the round's own KIND. Positive control: reverse the two stores and confirm the model goes RED. Extend to two concurrent senders for the `OWNER` handoff. | `crates/kernel/sched/src/membarrier/work.rs` — `KIND.store(..., Release)` then `PENDING.store(targets, Release)`; `service()` reads KIND only after an Acquire load of `PENDING` saw its own bit. The kind dispatch is `Kind::from_u32(KIND.load(Relaxed))`. | unowned |

| OPEN | DEFECT | MED | ext4 drops the `datasync` argument: `Ext4RegFileOps::fsync` and `Ext4StatFileOps::fsync` both take `_datasync` and discard it, so `fdatasync(2)` always pays a full mount-wide metadata commit plus an unconditional device barrier. The reference waits only on the inode's `i_datasync_tid` and skips the commit entirely when that transaction already committed, and issues the barrier only when its own commit is the one owing it. Three linked divergences: datasync ignored; the commit drains the whole mount's shadow map rather than this inode's transaction; the barrier is unconditional (a 4th flush on top of the 3 the WAL already issues) and ignores `nobarrier`. NOTE: gating the barrier on "did the commit write anything" is NOT a valid fix — it is only accidentally safe because `write_at_inner` unconditionally restages the inode block, so any future skip of that restage, or any data path not going through `write_at`, silently loses durability. The Linux-shaped fix needs a per-mount commit generation plus per-inode `i_sync_tid`/`i_datasync_tid` and a barrier watermark. | `crates/kernel/ext4/src/rootfs/inode/regular.rs:241`, `special.rs:284`, `ext4_sync_file` at `regular.rs:28-35`, `mount/batch.rs::commit_batch`. Reference: `commit_tid = datasync ? i_datasync_tid : i_sync_tid`. | unowned |

| OPEN | DEFECT | low | An io_uring region is one CONTIGUOUS buddy run; the reference allocates it as an unordered page vector reached through a kernel virtual mapping. ### F1220-acl-enforcement-disk

| Status | Class | Sev | Issue | Evidence | Owner |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| OPEN | MISSING | med | **`SB_POSIXACL` is set by no filesystem, and `SuperBlock::is_posixacl` has no consumer.** The reference gates the whole ACL path on it (`IS_POSIXACL`) — `acl_permission_check` skips the fetch, `posix_acl_create` returns without inheriting, `set_posix_acl`/`vfs_get_acl` answer EOPNOTSUPP. Here the flag exists, is unit-tested for its bit value, and is never raised; the ACL-capable decision is instead per-filesystem (f2fs's `acl` mount option, read inside its own inheritance path). F1220 deliberately did NOT gate enforcement on the flag for that reason — gating on a flag nobody sets would have made the fix dead on arrival, and gating tmpfs on it would have REGRESSED tmpfs, whose ACLs already enforced. Needs each ACL-capable filesystem to raise it from its own mount options and the generic paths to consult it, in one pass, so there is one truth. | F1220, measured: `grep -rn 'SB_POSIXACL\|is_posixacl' crates/` finds only the definition, the re-export, and `vfs/tests/superblock_flags.rs`. | — |
| OPEN | DEFECT | med | **Only f2fs rewrites its ACL on `chmod`; ext4, tmpfs and overlayfs do not.** The reference's `posix_acl_chmod` is called from each filesystem's `->setattr`, and it is what stops a `chmod` from changing the mode bits while the ACL keeps granting the access the caller just removed. With enforcement now live (this lane), a `chmod 0600` on an ext4 or tmpfs file carrying a named-user ACL still leaves that user's access intact — the mode says one thing and the check answers another. The generic halves exist: `vfs::posix_acl::chmod_masq` and `Inode::posix_acl_chmod`, both used by f2fs; each remaining filesystem needs the call at its own `setattr` plus its own store write. | F1220, measured: `grep -rn posix_acl_chmod crates/kernel` lists only `vfs/src/inode/acl.rs` and `f2fs/src/mount/prepare.rs`. Pinned for f2fs by `f2fs/src/tests/acl_enforce.rs::{a_chmod_narrows_the_acl_and_not_only_the_mode_bits,a_chmod_drops_an_acl_the_mode_bits_can_express_on_their_own}`; positive control: removing the call turns both RED. | — |
| OPEN | DEFECT | low | **The permission check has no RCU-walk equivalent, so filling the ACL cache can block inside a path walk.** The reference splits `check_acl` on `MAY_NOT_BLOCK`: an rcu-walk asks `get_cached_acl_rcu` and returns `-ECHILD` to force a ref-walk retry rather than calling into the filesystem. Oxide's walk is a ref walk only, so the first permission check on an inode may take the owning volume's lock and read a block from inside `path_lookup`. It is correct — no caller holds that lock across a permission check, and the result is cached per inode so it happens once — but it is a divergence, and it becomes a real problem the moment a non-blocking walk exists. | F1220. `vfs/src/namei/permission.rs::check_acl` has no `MAY_NOT_BLOCK` arm; `MAY_NOT_BLOCK` does not exist in the tree. The cache that makes it once-per-inode is `vfs/src/inode/acl.rs`. | — |
| FIXED B2269 | DEFECT | med | **`ext4::rootfs` preserves suspended quota state when RO→RW validation fails.** | B2269: `quota_remount_image` 6/6 and full ext4 suite green; negative control RED then restored. | B2269 |
| OPEN | DEFECT | med | **`syscalls` has six failing hosted tests on `main`.** Five in `affinity_abi::tests` (`cpumask_size_matches_linux_bitmap_size`, `getaffinity_rejects_a_buffer_too_small_for_the_kernel_mask`, `getaffinity_rejects_unaligned_len`, `getaffinity_returns_bytes_written_not_zero`, `setaffinity_accepts_a_short_len_and_zero_fills`) and one in `return_fastpath_tests`. The affinity set reports a 32-byte cpumask where the tests require 8, so they are reading a CPU count some other test in the same process installs — a shared-global-order defect, not an ABI one. | F1220, measured on main at `466a534f9`: `cargo test -p syscalls --no-fail-fast` → the same six, three runs in a row, byte-identical set in this lane's worktree. | — |
| FIXED B2268 | DEFECT | high | **ext4 has default-ACL inheritance.** The F1220 on-disk codec now receives the generic new-inode result at every allocating create path. Owner/mode preparation runs before the ACL decision, the ACL owns the umask decision, default is stored before access, and both writes join the open metadata transaction before `dir_link` or orphan enrollment. | B2268: `ext4/tests/acl_inherit_image.rs` has four image-level tests for regular, directory, tmpfile, FIFO, whiteout, symlink, path-helper, recursive, and failed-store behavior. `cargo test -p ext4 --no-fail-fast` has this suite green; its only failure is the already-open quota-remount row above. | B2268 |
| OPEN | INFRA | med | **`make lint-ratchet` is tree-wide red, so it blocks every unrelated push without distinguishing a branch's own regression.** The current result is 55 keys above its baseline (1967 findings across 107 unit+rule keys against a baseline total of 1702), including the three existing docs keys but also drivers, `sched`, `syscalls`, `mm-pmm`, `modules`, and `hal-x86_64`. The actual findings must be burned down by their owners; re-baselining would merely waive them. Until then the pre-push hook needs `SKIP_LINT_RATCHET=1`, which means a genuine regression can be hidden among the standing failures. | B2281: `make lint-ratchet` reports 55 `REGRESSION` lines, byte-identical on `origin/main`, and names no `crates/kernel/ext4` key, so this branch adds none. The prior three-doc finding report was stale. | unowned |

### C311-gate-diagnostics

| Status | Class | Sev | Issue | Evidence | Owner |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| OPEN | INFRA | med | **`make test-build-gate` names the crate that failed but not the compiler error, so a spurious failure reads exactly like a real one.** A push was rejected with `boot-aarch64` test targets failing to build; the underlying errors were `couldn't create a temp dir ... tgt/debug/deps/...` -- the target subdirectory had been deleted underneath the run by a concurrent build of the same branch. Disk was 56% used, so not space. A lane reading only the gate's summary line would have gone off fixing `boot-aarch64`'s features and dependencies for a defect that does not exist. The gate should surface the first underlying error, and a vanished-target-dir failure should be distinguishable from a compile failure. | B2256, reported by the lane after its branch merged as `bbadf81aa`. Same family as the vanished-worktree gate log recorded for F1213, where `stack-gate` printed a plausible over-budget verdict with a full 20-path table while the build's own source tree had been removed. | unowned |
| OPEN | INFRA | med | **`git push ... \| tail` reports the pipeline's exit status, not git's, so a rejected push reads as a successful one.** Two lanes did this in one day. B2256 reported a push as "in its pre-push gates" when git had already been rejected by `test-build-gate`; B2252's readahead sibling reported `exit 0` from `git push ... \| tail -30` while the push had failed the stack gate, and only caught it by reading the log body. The fix in a lane's own habits is `${PIPESTATUS[0]}` or no pipe at all, and to verify a landed push by `git fetch` plus a SHA comparison rather than by any command's status. Worth a note in the git-workflow section of `CLAUDE.md` since it has now produced two false completion reports. | B2256 and the F1212 lane, both 2026-08-17. The general rule already exists as "Trust the run's exit status, NEVER a `/tmp` log you found by timestamp" and as "Never state a conclusion a proxy cannot support"; this is the same class with a pipeline as the proxy. | unowned |
| OPEN | MISSING | med | **No per-packet security label exists, so `SCM_SECURITY` on a received INET datagram can never report one and `SO_PEERSEC` on a TCP socket only ever reports "unlabelled".** The reference derives a datagram's peer label from the packet via NetLabel (CIPSO/CALIPSO) or an IPsec security association, and sets a connected INET socket's peer label at `inet_conn_established` from the same source. Neither source exists here: no NetLabel, no labelled IPsec. AF_UNIX is unaffected, which is why the session bus works. | B2262, established while wiring `SO_PEERSEC`: `selinux_socket_getpeersec_dgram` needs `selinux_skb_peerlbl_sid`, and `selinux_inet_conn_established` needs `asoc->peer_secid`. Our `syscalls/src/recvmsg/inet.rs::peer_label` now correctly reports nothing rather than mis-reporting a stream peer's label. | unowned |
| OPEN | MISSING | low | **A socket's label is taken from the creating task without consulting a policy type-transition for the socket's class.** The reference computes it as `sockcreate_sid` if the task staged one, else a `type_transition` on the socket's security class from the task's own label. Ours uses the staged label or the task's label directly, which is the same answer with no policy loaded and the same answer for any policy that declares no socket type-transition — the ordinary case — but diverges for a policy that does. The class is not available at the one construction funnel where the label is taken, which is why it is not yet consulted. | B2262, `net/src/sock/construct.rs::new_owned` calls `security::network::new_socket_label()`, and `selinux-runtime/src/network.rs::create_sid` returns the staged-or-own label; the reference's `socket_sockcreate_sid` calls `security_transition_sid(sid, sid, secclass, NULL, &socksid)`. | unowned |
| OPEN | COVERAGE | med | **Nothing fails if the socket-labelling module is never installed at boot.** `net::selinux_glue::init()` is called from one place in `kmain/src/kmain/early.rs`; deleting that call leaves every hosted test green (each installs the module itself) and every arch build green, and `SO_PEERSEC` silently returns to answering ENOPROTOOPT — the exact defect B2262 fixed. The same hole covers the two label-source installers beside it. A boot-reachability check is needed, not another unit test. | B2262. Confirmed by construction: the wiring tests in `net/src/sock_opts/peersec/tests.rs` call `install_socket_label` directly, so they cannot observe the boot path. | unowned |
| OPEN | DEFECT | high | **Something decrements `preempt_count` once more than it incremented it, wrapping the count one below zero — the seed of the B2264 livelock, still unattributed.** The scheduler now repairs the count instead of livelocking on it, so the symptom is survivable, but the unpaired decrement is a real accounting bug: while the count is wrapped, `should_resched()` and `preemptible()` both read a non-zero word, so that CPU cannot take a reschedule at any preempt-enable until a `schedule()` repairs it. Intermittent: the F1241 post-merge x86 smoke named `local_bh_enable` subtracting `0x200` from zero at `sched/src/bh.rs`, while its retry and F1242's final x86 boot passed. | B2264 added the pre-subtract detector. F1242 established that the reported `bh.rs` line is the generic `spin_unlock_bh` funnel, not the acquisition: `LockBhGuard::drop` discarded its held-lock trace before calling the funnel. It now checks the pair while that trace is live, so the next recurrence prints the exact `rank@file:line` acquisition before the existing detector reports the subtract. Positive control removed the pre-enable check and failed (`0` observed versus outstanding depth `1`); restored test passes. Linux 7.2-rc4 performs the same per-handler preempt-count snapshot and restoration as this tree, so that restoration is not itself the divergence. F1242 final smoke: x86 first attempt 46 s, ARM first attempt 56 s, both serial RX; no recurrence, so no root-cause fix is claimed. | unowned |
